### T061fc06d787d17ceb28a4001

Text hash 2b0b961f5170990963d4a3c952d1382d9c00f9c017febb11f135c91f74660a3d; absolute characters 0–680; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8252-exemption-samuel-j-gompers-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Samuel J. Gompers, Chief Clerk, Department of Labor, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 7958 of August 19, 1938, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said Samuel J. Gompers from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1940.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

September 14, 1939.

```

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Harry T. Edwards, senior technologist, Bureau of Plant Industry, Department of Agriculture, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for periods totaling two years by Executive Orders No. 8259 of September 21, 1939, and No. 8525 of August 26, 1940, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said Harry T. Edwards from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending October 31, 1941.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

July 24, 1941.

```

### T0af4cac95470471ac34e3246

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Robert F. Whitehead, Solicitor, Patent Office, Department of Commerce, who, during the current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of four months:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Robert F. Whitehead from compulsory retirement for age for a period of four months ending June 30, 1939.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

February 15, 1939.

```

September 28, 1940.

by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat . 382, 404 (U.
```

### T2ce556d601656fea18e3dd99

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Alphonso L. Graham, assistant storekeeper, Division of Purchase and Supply, United States Maritime Commission, who, during the month of June, 1941, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Alphonso L. Graham from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending June 30, 1942.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

May 2, 1941.

```

Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that James E. The White House,

June 25, 1941.

Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Arthur W. Exline, senior power plant operative, Government Printing Office, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 8609 of December 11, 1940, be further exempted from such compulsory retirement for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby further exempt the said Arthur W. Exline from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending December 31, 1942.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

December 11, 1941.

```

### T877ae4652d30aa5dc4af1a9b

Text hash 08f8bfd4590fed61304c7049c0e0b262806c46d1dad685598f98f590db75d6fc; absolute characters 0–723; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-8256-exemption-maurice-c-latta-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Maurice C. Latta, Executive Clerk, The White House Office, Executive Office of the President, who, during the month of October, 1939, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Maurice C. Latta from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending October 31, 1940.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

September 20, 1939.

```

June 20, 1940.

```text
Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Rudolph Forster, Executive Clerk, The White House Office, who, during the month of October, 1942, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, and Maurice C. Latta, Executive Clerk, The White House Office, who has heretofore been exempted from compulsory retirement for age until October 31, 1942, be exempted and further exempted, respectively, from compulsory retirement for age:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), the said Rudolph Forster and Maurice C. Latte are hereby exempted and further exempted, respectively, from compulsory retirement for age.

FRANKLIN D ROOSEVELT

The White House,

September 3, 1942.

```

Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that James E. Harper, Chief, Division of Appointments, Treasury Department, who, during the month of June, 1939, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to him, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said James E. Harper from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending June 30, 1940.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

May 19, 1939.

```

THE PRESIDENT 13419
Executive Order No. 11314
CREATING A BOARD OF INQUIRY TO REPORT ON CERTAIN LABOR
DISPUTES AFFECTING THE MILITARY JET ENGINE INDUSTRY, MILI­
TARY AIRCRAFT INDUSTRY, MILITARY ARMAMENT INDUSTRY AND
MILITARY ELECTRONICS INDUSTRY OF THE UNITED STATES
W H E R E A S, there have existed certain labor disputes between Gen­
eral Electric Company and certain of its employees represented by : 
International Union of Electrical Radio and Machine Workers, 
A F L -C IO ; International Association of Machinists and Aerospace 
Workers, A F L -C IO ; International Union, United Automobile, Aero­
space and A gricultural Implement Workers of America, A F L -C IO  ; 
International Union, Allied Industrial W orkers of America, A F L - 
CIO ; International Brotherhood of Electrical Workers, A F L -C IO ; 
Sheet Metal W orkers’  International Association, A F L -C IO ; Ameri­
can Federation of Technical Engineers, A F L -C IO  ; American F lint 
Glass W orkers’  Union of North America, A F L -C IO ; United 
Electrical Radio and Machine W orkers of America; United Associa­
tion of Journeymen and Apprentices of the Plumbing and Pipe F itting 
Industry of the United States and Canada, A F L -C IO  ; United Steel­
workers of America, A F L -C IO ; International Union of Operating 
Engineers, A F L -C IO  ; International Brotherhood of Teamsters, 
Chauffeurs, Warehousemen and Helpers of America ; Pattern Makers’  
League of North America, A F L -C IO  ; International Brotherhood of 
Firemen and Oilers, A F L -C IO ; Syracuse Draftsmens Union; and
W H E R E A S, although a tentative resolution of these disputes was 
arrived at by the parties, or some of them, nevertheless there is a strike 
at the Evendale, Ohio, plant of General Electric Company by Local 
No. 647 of the International Union of the United Automobile, Aero­
space and Agricultural Implement Workers of America, A F L -C IO , 
and Locals 34 and 912 of the International Association of Machinists 
and Aerospace Workers, A F L -C IO , and there exists a threat that 
there will be further strikes at General Electric Company plants by 
one or more of the aforesaid international unions or local units 
thereof ; and
W H E R E A S, the existing strike at the Evendale, Ohio, plant of 
General Electric Company will, in my opinion, if permitted to con­
tinue, affect a substantial p art of the m ilitary jet engine industry, 
which industry is engaged in trade, commerce, transportation, trans­
mission, or communication among the several states or with foreign 
nations, or engaged in the production of goods for commerce, and which 
strike, if permitted to continue, will imperil the national safety ; and
W H E R E A S, threatened strikes at other General Electric Company 
plants will, in my opinion, if allowed to occur, affect a substantial 
p art of the military jet engine industry, m ilitary aircraft industry, 
m ilitary armament industry and m ilitary electronics industry, which 
industries are engaged in trade, commerce, transportation, transmis­
sion, or communication among the several states or with foreign na­
tions, or engaged in the production of goods for commerce, and which 
threatened strikes, if permitted to occur, will imperil the national 
safety ;
NOW T H E R E F O R E  by virtue of the authority vested in me by 
Section 206 of the Labor Management Relations Act of 1947 (61 Stat. 
155; 29 U.S.C. 176), I  hereby create a Board of Inquiry, consisting 
of Mr. David L. Cole, Chairman, Mr. John Dunlop and Mr. Jacob 
Seidenberg, whom I  appoint to inquire into the issues involved in 
these disputes.
FEDERAL REGISTER, VOL. 31, NO. 202— TUESDAY, OCTOBER 18, 1966

=== PDF page 12 of 52 ===
13420 THE PRESIDENT
The Board shall have powers and duties as set forth in Title I I  of 
such Act. The Board shall report to the President in accordance 
with the provisions of Section 206 of such Act. W ith respect to the 
dispute giving rise to the strike at Evendale, Ohio, plant it shall report 
on or before October 18th, 1966, and with respect to the dispute or 
disputes resulting in any other such strike, it shall report as soon as 
possible.
Upon the submission of its report or reports, the Board shall con­
tinue in existence to perform such other functions as may be required 
under such Act.
L yndon  B. J ohnson
T h e W h it e H ouse ,
October 17 ,  1966.
[F.R. Doc. 66-11415; Filed, Oct. IT, 1966; 1:07 p.m.]
FEDERAL REGISTER, VOL. 31, NO. 202— TUESDAY, OCTOBER 18, 1966

```

Whereas, in my judgment, the public interest requires that Mrs. Frances S. Nichols, Editorial Assistant, Bureau of American Ethnology, Smithsonian Institution, who, during the month of August, 1939, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to her, be exempted from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S.C., title 5, sec. 715a), I hereby exempt the said Mrs. Frances S. Nichols from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending August 31, 1940.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

July 17, 1939.

The text is a transcription (grade mirror_transcription) and the original is not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends tested: express revocation, amendment or supersession by a later order; repeal of section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932 before June 30, 1939; and the employee's separation. The packet's U.S. Code and eCFR searches (checked 2026-10-17) found no hit for this order. No held source shows a repeal or change of section 204 before June 30, 1939, and a general statute that did not name this order would be only a disclosed candidate under the owner ruling on unnamed instruments, not an end. The held record contains no separation or death date for the employee and none is needed: the permission was stated for a fixed period, so the stated end is the earliest end the held evidence supports.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains a single operative command, the exemption itself; the recital is the President's stated judgment on which the exemption rests and is not a separate command. Text and identity: the held text is the American Presidency Project transcription (grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (common-crawl capture) has the heading \"Executive Order 8142—Exemption of Thomas A. Jaggar, Jr., From Compulsory Retirement For Age\", which names this order number, its date (1939-05-25) agrees with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. I read the whole text: one recital and one operative sentence, so the clause inventory is the single whole-paragraph clause. The sentence \"hereby further exempt the said Thomas A. Jaggar, Jr., from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending July 31, 1940\" fixes the end of the exemption on July 31, 1940; that is the first end, with the exclusive reading disclosed. The packet's later-events search (Federal Register disposition notes of 6938 catalog records from 1937 onward, plus operative sentences in 17521 saved order texts) returned one hit, a \"See\" cross-reference between this order and the earlier exemption order EO 7936, which predates it. The packet's name search of held order texts for the person named in this order is also cited. Other leads and later events are discussed in the clause reason. No held source shows an earlier end (revocation, supersession, or an earlier separation or death of the employee), so none is claimed.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Earlier end than July 31, 1940 tested: The packet's later-events search (Federal Register disposition notes of 6938 catalog records from 1937 onward, plus operative sentences in 17521 saved order texts) returned one hit, a \\\"See\\\" cross-reference between this order and the earlier exemption order EO 7936, which predates it. No held instrument revokes, supersedes or terminates this exemption before its stated date. No agency or Civil Service Commission record of the employee's separation or death is held, so no earlier end is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order directs nothing besides the exemption: no report, appointment, transfer or other independent command appears in its text.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption let Thomas A. Jaggar, Jr., stay in service beyond the retirement age; service rendered while it was in force is an accrued effect that the expiry does not undo. The order contains no continuing command of its own after July 31, 1940; the statute it cites (section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404) is its authority only.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order is a one-person order about Thomas A. Jaggar, Jr.,; its identity rests on the number, date and subject in the APP page heading and the held text. Its whole text is one recital and one operative sentence, so one whole-paragraph clause is the complete inventory, and the stated period \\\"one year ending July 31, 1940\\\" is the only duration in Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (common-crawl capture) has the heading \"Executive Order 8224—Exemption of John P. Dunlop From Compulsory Retirement for Age\", which names this order number, its date (1939-08-24) agrees with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. I read the whole text: one recital and one operative sentence, so the clause inventory is the single whole-paragraph clause. The sentence \"hereby exempt the said John P. Dunlop from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1940\" fixes the end of the exemption on September 30, 1940; that is the first end, with the exclusive reading disclosed. The packet's later-events search (Federal Register disposition notes of 6938 catalog records from 1937 onward, plus operative sentences in 17521 saved order texts) returned no hit. The packet's name search of held order texts for the person named in this order is also cited. Other leads and later events are discussed in the clause reason. Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/MIRROR_DOCUMENTS.jsonl (common-crawl capture) has the heading \"Executive Order 8256—Exemption of Maurice C. Latta From Compulsory Retirement for Age\", which names this order number, its date (1939-09-20) agrees with the held text, and its text_sha256 equals the held file's. I read the whole text: one recital and one operative sentence, so the clause inventory is the single whole-paragraph clause. The sentence \"hereby exempt the said Maurice C. Latta from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending October 31, 1940\" fixes the end of the exemption on October 31, 1940; that is the first end, with the exclusive reading disclosed. The packet's later-events search (Federal Register disposition notes of 6938 catalog records from 1937 onward, plus operative sentences in 17521 saved order texts) returned two hits, \"See\" cross-references to the later further-exemption orders EO 8497 and EO 8904. The packet's name search of held order texts for the person named in this order is also cited. Other leads and later events are discussed in the clause reason. No held source shows an earlier end (revocation, supersession, or an earlier separation or death of the employee), so none is claimed.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Earlier end than October 31, 1940 tested: The packet's later-events search (Federal Register disposition notes of 6938 catalog records from 1937 onward, plus operative sentences in 17521 saved order texts) returned two hits, \\\"See\\\" cross-references to the later further-exemption orders EO 8497 and EO 8904. No agency or Civil Service Commission record of the employee's separation or death is held, so no earlier end is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order directs nothing besides the exemption: no report, appointment, transfer or other independent command appears in its text.\",\"savings_check\":\"The exemption let Maurice C. Latta stay in service beyond the retirement age; service rendered while it was in force is an accrued effect that the expiry does not undo. The order contains no continuing command of its own after October 31, 1940; the statute it cites (section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404) is its authority only.\",\"scope_check\":\"The order is a one-person order about Maurice C. the APP page record with the same sha256 names order number 8392, the date 1940-04-12 and the subject, and its text hash is the held text's hash. The order is a short whole document: a recital paragraph, one operative paragraph and the signature and date line. Inventory: the one operative paragraph is the only clause; the recital is the President's finding that the public interest requires the exemption and commands nothing. End: the order exempts Zeke Johnson from compulsory retirement for age for a term it states as 'for a period of one year ending April 30, 1941', so it ends by its own terms on 1941-04-30 (inclusive reading; the exclusive reading ends the term when that day begins). Later instruments: the packet's later-events search of Office of the Federal Register disposition notes (1937 onward), its person-name search of held order texts, and its current-law searches of the U.S. Code and the eCFR were selected after reading their coverage notes. The packet lookup of held texts that cite this order by number found none. The claim rests on a transcription and carries the standing note that the original is not yet held and the quotation must be re-checked when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No held text revokes, supersedes or shortens this exemption before its stated end. The later-events search returned no hit for this order and the number-citation lookup of held texts found no citing order..\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The operative paragraph holds one command, the exemption of the named person. The recital paragraph is a finding and no report, delegation, rule or second command appears in the held text.\",\"savings_check\":\"The held text has no savings clause and no clause continuing any effect past the stated term; the term is the only duration the text gives.\",\"scope_check\":\"The whole order is a single clause with no partial, conditional or severable part, and no amendment to its text is held. The recital cites the earlier exemption order Executive Order 8094 of April 19, 1939 as predecessor; it precedes this order and are not events affecting The page record's heading names the number and its date and subject agree with the text. Its only operative command, under section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, exempts Nelson S. Thompson from compulsory retirement for age for a stated term: \"I hereby exempt the said Nelson S. Thompson from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending September 30, 1941.\". The term ends September 30, 1941 by its own words. Later exemption orders for the same person are later events and new grants; none revokes or shortens this order.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end is held. Whole-order status is no (provisional, already adopted; not changed here). The order exempts Albert Clyde-Burton from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending July 31, 1941 and ends on that stated date. The end is the order's own fixed term, recorded inclusive with the exclusive reading disclosed. No later event is held. Whole-order status is no (provisional, already adopted; not changed here). The order exempts James L. Hughes from compulsory retirement for age for a period of one year ending November 30, 1941 and ends on that stated date. Identity: the held text is the American Presidency Project page (node 371610, Common Crawl capture 2024-12-05T06:44:19Z); the page record with the same sha256 has a heading naming Executive Order 8610, the date 1940-12-11 that matches the signature date in the text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file. The text was read in full: one recital paragraph, one operative sentence, signature and date. Inventory: a single clause, the exemption of Miss Roberta L. Lindsey. End: the stated period gives 1941-12-31 as the first end by the order's own terms. EO 8995, signed December 26, 1941, cites this order by number (\"Executive Order No. 8610 of \") and exempts the same employee again: \"I hereby further exempt\" ... \"for a period of one year ending December 31, 1942\". It states no separate effective date, so its signature date is recorded as the event date. It is a later event, not an end: it is a new exemption for a later period and does not revoke, shorten or supersede the exemption in EO 8610. The packet's machine searches (U.S. Code release point 119-121, eCFR as of 2026-10-14, OFR catalog, and held-text person-name search) record no hit that revokes, supersedes or shortens the order. The record rests on a transcription: original not yet held; replace and re-check quotes when an official copy is retrieved.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end found. The order fixes its own period ending 1941-12-31; the packet's U.S. Code, eCFR and OFR-catalog machine searches record no hit that ends it earlier. The only later order citing EO 8610 is EO 8995, an extension that does not revoke it. Nothing in the held evidence shows the exemption ended before December 31, 1941.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The recital states the public-interest finding and the operative sentence is the only command; the signature and date add none. No independent command survives the stated end.\",\"savings_check\":\"Checked the order for a savings or continuing provision: none appears in the held text.
